SOTA LP likely designed to optimize arbitrary nonnegative continuous sequence and should find near 1.5. We need implement without huge LP. Maybe we can use the LP but on reduced sequence or exploit FFT+custom solver.

Let's investigate the SOTA LP more. It iterates f -> g where g maximizes sum under conv(f,g)<=M. This is a linear program with convolution constraint. It might be solvable via a simple water-filling algorithm because the constraint matrix has a "network matrix" structure? The convolution of f (nonnegative) with g: (f*g)[k] = sum_i f_i g_{k-i}. This is a linear constraint. The feasible set {g>=0: f*g <= M} is a polymatroid if f is nonnegative? Actually constraints over all k. The objective maximize sum g. This is like maximizing a linear function over a transportation/packing polytope. Since f is a kernel, constraints are sliding weighted sums. If f is arbitrary nonnegative length n, can solve via min-cost flow? Let's model: variable g_i. Constraint for sum k: sum_{i} f_i g_{k-i} <= M. Let h_k = (f*g)_k. This is a convolution inequality. The dual constraints are weighted window sums. This is a one-dimensional total variation? Maybe dual is a shortest path.

The dual: min M * sum y_k
s.t. for all i, (f_rev * y)_i? >=1? Specifically sum_{t=0}^{n-1} f_t y_{i+t} >=1, 0<=i<=n-1, y_k>=0.
This is: given nonnegative sequence f of length n, find minimal sum y such that sliding weighted sums with kernel f are at least 1. This is a continuous linear program (covering). The constraint matrix has consecutive ones with weights f_t, but weights vary with offset. Is there a greedy optimal algorithm? For covering with a nonnegative FIR filter, minimum L1 norm input y to make output y*f_bar >= 1 over a window. This is like "minimum norm control" and can be solved by linear programming; not trivial greedy unless f all ones.

But n=6733, dual has 13465 variables and 6733 constraints. Could solve with scipy linprog using dense? 13M entries? Actually matrix is (n) x (2n-1) with each row n nonzeros ->91M. Too big dense. Sparse: 91M floats ~728MB maybe too high; plus SciPy sparse overhead huge (>2GB). Not viable.

Can use first-order methods on dual/primal because objective simple and constraints separable? We can solve the LP approximately using ADMM or projected gradient. The LP is convex. We just need a decent direction g; doesn't need exact optimum. SOTA may still work with approximate g.

Alternatively, we can implement SOTA primal LP via a simple greedy heuristic: choose g to satisfy constraints while maximizing sum. For fixed f, constraints are linear. We can propose g_i proportional to something? Maybe g_i = 1 / (f_i?)? Let's see.

If we take y for dual: choose y_k = alpha constant. Constraint: alpha * sum_{t=0}^{n-1} f_t >=1 => alpha=1/S. Objective M*2n-1/S. Primal dual bound. The corresponding primal? Maybe g_i = M/S? Then conv(f,g)=g S? If g constant M/S, each constraint =M? sum g = nM/S. Is that optimal? Not necessarily. For constant f, S=n, g=M/n constant, sum=M. Is primal optimal? For f constant, constraint conv(f,g)[k]=sum_i g_i (partial) <=M, maximize sum g_i. With nonnegative, constant g_i=M/n; edge constraints partial sum for k=0: g0<=M, but full sum constraint at center S_g? Actually center conv = sum_i g_i <=M. Max sum =M by setting sum=M. Constant gives M. Good.

For nonconstant f, maybe use g_i = M / W_i where W_i = max_k sum_{valid} f_{k-i}? Not sure.

Thus we can do smooth gradient descent efficiently:
- Normalize a sum=1, a>=0.
- Compute c=conv(a,a) via FFT.
- Choose T (temperature) to approximate max. w=exp(T*(c - max)) (stable), Z=sum w.
- Gradient dL/da_i = 2 * h_i / Z (where h_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}). Let's check: ∂L/∂a_i = (1/Z) sum_k w_k ∂c[k]/∂a_i = (2/Z) sum_k w_k a_{k-i} = 2 h_i/Z. Good.
- We want minimize L subject to sum a=1, a>=0. Use projected gradient or mirror descent.
Update: a = a - eta * gradient, project to simplex (nonnegative sum1). Or multiplicative mirror descent: a *= exp(-eta gradient), then normalize. This stays positive. Since gradient at constant? c triangular, weights concentrated at center k=n-1, h_i = a_{n-1-i}=constant, gradient uniform; with sum constraint multiplicative update does nothing (because gradient uniform -> exp(-eta uniform) constant). Fast score:
```
def fft_conv_same(a, out=None):
    n=len(a); N=1<<ceil_log2(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1]
    return c
```
For many calls, precompute N globally? Since n fixed. But propose_candidate may choose n. We can cache N in function attributes? Top-level helper no closures but can use global state? - Mirror update: a = a * np.exp(-eta * g). But g scale can be large. We can use adaptive eta.

Because mirror descent on simplex: a_new = exp(log a - eta g). If eta too large, may distort. We can use `eta = step / (np.linalg.norm(g) / n)`? For gradient descent in Euclidean, eta ~ 1/L. Alternatively use exponent small: a *= exp(-gamma * g_normalized) where g_normalized = (g - g.mean())/(g.max()-g.min()+eps), gamma=0.1. This scale-independent. Since adding mean doesn't change after normalization, use centered gradient. Update: a *= exp(-gamma * (g - g_mean)); normalize. gamma controls step. This is multiplicative central gradient descent. It preserves sum after normalization. If gamma too small slow; can decay/increase.
We can implement:
```
gn = g - g.mean()
gn = gn / (np.std(gn)+1e-12)
cand = a * np.exp(-gamma * gn)
cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-15); cand /= cand.sum()
```
But if gn std=0, no change. gamma schedule from 0.05 to0.5. Since g entries O(1)? g = 2h/Z; h sum? h ~ ... Z~? Could be O(1), std maybe small. Normalize makes step gamma in units of std. Good.

Fast score using FFT with normalization:
```
def fast_score(a):
    n=len(a)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0.01: return np.inf
    N=1
    while N<2*n-1: N*=2
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1]
    return float(2.0*n*np.max(c)/(s*s))
```
Potential numerical negative tiny in c; max fine.

Mirror step function:
```
def smooth_loss_and_grad(a, T):
    n=len(a); N=...; F=...; c=...
    cm=c.max()
    if T <=0:
       w=np.ones_like(c); Z=len(c)
    else:
       # to avoid overflow, x=T*(c-cm); min maybe -T*cm, if cm huge and T large underflow fine.
       x=T*(c-cm)
       # clip x to >= -745 to avoid warnings? exp(-1000)=0 no warning? Use np.exp(x, out=...)
       w=np.exp(x)
       Z=w.sum()
       if Z==0: w[:]=0; w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1; Z=1
    loss = cm + np.log(Z)/T if T>0 else np.log(Z)
    # gradient h
    N2=1
    while N2 < 3*n-2: N2*=2
    ar = a[::-1]
    F_ar=np.fft.rfft(ar,N2)
    F_w=np.fft.rfft(w,N2)
    h=np.fft.irfft(F_ar*F_w,N2)[n-1:2*n-1]
    g=2.0*h/Z
    return loss, c, cm, w, g
```
Memory ok.

But in each iteration, computing N2 FFT large. Could combine? A = rfft(a), W=rfft(w). We already have rfft(a) of size Nc (16384) for c. For h need size N2=32768; can zero-pad or use full N2. We could compute F_a_N2 once from a via rfft(a,N2) (instead of Nc) and derive c by multiplying F_a_N2*F_a_N2 and taking first 2n-1, because FFT size 32768 also sufficient for linear conv (>=2n-1). So use single FFT size N=32768 for both c and h. That simplifies and may be okay (32768 instead of 16384 + 32768, saves one FFT). c from N=32768 also correct. So each iteration: rfft(a,N) once, c=irfft(F*F,N), w, rfft(w,N), h=irfft(F*F_w? Wait h uses ar (a reversed) not same F. Need rfft(ar,N). That's second FFT. So two forward FFTs (a and ar and w?) Update:
```
def mirror_update(a,g,gamma):
    gm=g.mean()
    sd=np.std(g-gm)
    if sd < 1e-15: return a.copy()
    z=(g-gm)/sd
    # To avoid excessive negative, clip z to [-10,10]
    z=np.clip(z,-10,10)
    cand=a*np.exp(-gamma*z)
    cand=np.maximum(cand,1e-15)
    cand=cand/cand.sum()
    return cand
```
But if g has huge dynamic, z clipping. Uses std = population. Need scale of g maybe. If g has zeros? okay.

```
scale=np.max(np.abs(g-gm)) or 1
z=(g-gm)/scale
cand=a*np.exp(-gamma*z)
```

```
def optimize_subgrad(a0, deadline, rng, Ls=(1,2,3,5,8), gamma_schedule...):
    a=a0.copy(); S=a.sum(); a/=S*target? (We'll maintain sum S0)
    best=a0; best_score=fast_score
    no_improve=0
    while time<deadline:
       # choose L maybe cycling/random
       c=conv_fft(a)
       max_c=c.max(); score=...
       if score<best_score: best...
       # choose gamma decay: start 0.2 reduce over iterations
       # update:
       idx = top L (argpartition)
       g=zeros
       for k in idx: segment add
       # center/scale
       gm=g.mean(); sd=np.max(np.abs(g-gm))
       if sd>0:
          z=(g-gm)/sd
          z=np.clip(z,-5,5)
          gamma = base / (1+0.01*it)
          cand=a*np.exp(-gamma*z)
          cand[cand<min]=min
          cand[cand>max]=max? (not necessary)
          cand/=cand.sum()*S0? If we maintain sum=1, cand/=cand.sum()
       else: break
       # If cand score (periodic) > threshold, maybe accept? We can just set a=cand.
       a=cand
       it++
       if no improve > N: reset a=best.copy() and perturb
    return best
```

Implement:
```
def top_L_proposal(a, c, L, gamma, rng):
    n=len(a)
    M=2*n-1
    # choose top L via argpartition; if L=1 use argmax
    if L <=1: idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
    else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -L)[-L:]
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k); m=hi-lo+1
       if m>0:
          # a[k-k? for j lo..hi: a[k-j] where k-lo..hi? We need segment a[k-hi : k-lo+1] reversed? Let's derive.
          # For i from lo to hi, g[i] += 2*a[k-i]. The indices k-i go from k-lo down to k-hi. That's a slice a[k-hi : k-lo+1] reversed.
          vals = a[k-hi : k-lo+1][::-1]
          g[lo:hi+1] += 2*vals
    # center/scale
    gm=g.mean()
    scale=np.max(np.abs(g-gm))
    if scale < 1e-15: return a.copy(), 0.0
    z=(g-gm)/scale
    z=np.clip(z, -6, 6)
    cand=a*np.exp(-gamma*z)
    cand=np.maximum(cand, 1e-15)
    # rescale to maintain sum (maybe target sum)
    S=a.sum()
    cand *= S/cand.sum()
    return cand, scale
```
Check slice: n=4, k=3, lo=0,hi=3, k-hi=0,k-lo+1=4 => a[0:4][::-1]=[a3,a2,a1,a0]. g[i]+=2*a[3-i] -> g0=2a3,g1=2a2,g2=2a1,g3=2a0 correct. If k=0, lo=0,hi=0,k-hi=0,k-lo+1=1 => a0, g0=2a0 correct.

Gamma size: scale uses max abs of centered g/g maybe. z in [-6,6]. gamma can be 0.05 to1. If gamma=1, exp range e^6 huge; may make some zeros. Use clip z ±3 maybe. Let z=np.clip(z,-3,3); gamma 0.1-0.5 => exp factor up to e^1.5=4.5. Good.

L schedule: Use a list [1,1,2,3,5,8] and choose based on iteration: e.g., L = Ls[ it % len(Ls) ] plus random larger if no improve. Or adapt: if score improves, keep L; if not, increase L.

Gamma schedule: basis `gamma = gamma_start * (0.995^it)`? Need enough movement. Could use decaying from 0.5 to0.05 over 100k iterations: `gamma = 0.05 + 0.45 * (1/(1+0.0005*it))`. Also random variation: `gamma * (0.5+0.5*rng.random())`.

Simulated annealing temperature: score difference can be small. Use `temp = temp_start * (temp_end/temp_start)^progress` maybe. But if we greedily accept only improvements, simpler. Let's do acceptance: if cand_score <= cur_score accept; else accept if `rng.random() < math.exp(-(cand_score-cur_score)/temp)`. temp start maybe 0.005, end 1e-5. Scores around1.5, differences small. But if bad candidate diff10, reject. Need avoid overflow. Use `if diff < temp*50`.

However accepting worse may waste; but helps escape. We can also if no improvement for `restart_iters`, reset to best and perturb.

Top-L proposal from `cur_c` after reset. If current best is constant, top1 k center, but a perturbed random, gradient not uniform; may move.

Portfolio runner:
```
def run_topL_search(a0, deadline, rng, label):
   a=normalize(a0,S0); S0 target
   cur_c=conv(a); cur_score=...
   best=a; best_score=...
   it=0; no_imp=0
   while time < deadline:
      # proposal
      L = choose L
      gamma=...
      cand, scale = topL_proposal(a, cur_c,...)
      cand_c=conv(cand); cand_score=...
      diff=cand_score-cur_score
      accept=False
      if diff <=1e-12: accept=True
      else: temp=...
             if -diff/temp < 50: accept = rng.random() < exp(-diff/temp)
      if accept:
          a=cand; cur_c=cand_c; cur_score=cand_score
          if cur_score < best_score: best_score=...; best=a.copy(); no_imp=0
          else no_imp+=1
      else: no_imp+=1
      if no_imp > 2000:
          a = perturb(best, sigma=0.2+0.3*rng)
          cur_c=conv(a); cur_score=...
          no_imp=0
      it+=1
      if it % 5000: print
   return best, best_score
```

Need implement LP build efficiently.
```
def build_constraint_matrix(f, L,R):
    n=len(f); m=2*n-1
    pf=np.cumsum(f, dtype=np.float64); pf=np.concatenate(([0],pf))
    k=np.arange(m, dtype=np.int64)
    A=np.empty((m,B), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(B):
       lo = np.maximum(0, k - R[i])
       hi = np.minimum(n-1, k - L[i])
       valid = lo <= hi
       # For invalid remains 0; for valid compute
       A[valid, i] = pf[hi[valid]+1] - pf[lo[valid]]
    return A
```
This uses k arrays each loop; optimize: precompute k. For each block, `kk=k` still. `lo = k - R; lo[lo<0]=0` but creates arrays. We can use `np.maximum(0, k - R[i], out=lo_buf)` etc. To reduce allocations, allocate lo,hi,valid each loop? Fine B=400.
Alternative compute window sums via sliding: For block length b and start L, the sequence S_k is convolution of f with rectangular window; can be computed by `np.convolve(f, np.ones(b))` shifted? That would be O(n*b) if naive. Prefix is good.

We can store A as Fortran order? linprog likes contiguous. `A = np.empty((m,B), dtype=np.float64)` fill columns; row-major, column fill non-contiguous but okay. Could fill row-wise? For each row k, compute all block window sums? Maybe B vector operation per row: lo = k - R_arr; hi = k - L_arr; valid; A[k, valid]=pf[hi+1]-pf[lo]. This would be m iterations (13k) with B=400 vector ops, perhaps more overhead. Column loop 400 with vector length13k better.

`next_pow2(m)`:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    N=1
    while N<m: N <<=1
    return N
```
For n=6733, pow2.

`fft_convolution(a)`:
```
def fft_convolution(a):
    n=len(a); m=2*n-1
    N=1
    while N<m: N <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)[:m]
    # avoid tiny negative? max unaffected; but for building M use max
    return c
```
Could use `np.maximum(c,0)`? For score max, negative tiny irrelevant; for LP M max positive. Use `np.where(c>0,c,0)` maybe copies. Not needed.

`score_from_c(a,c)`:
```
s=a.sum()
if s<0.01: return np.inf
return float(2.0*len(a)*float(np.max(c))/(s*s))
```
If all c negative due numeric? max maybe 0.

`normalize_sum(a, s)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float)
a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
tot=a.sum()
if tot<=0: a=np.ones_like(a); tot=float(n)
return a*(s/tot)
```

`make_starts(n, S0, rng)`:
Use vectorized. Ensure length n.
List generation with low memory (all length n). Maybe 20 starts, each 6733 -> fine.
```
def generate_initials(n, S0, rng):
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    out=[]
    def add(a,name):
       a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.clip(a,1e-6,1e6); out.append(normalize_sum(a,S0))
    # ramps
    for lam in [-8,-3,-1.5,-0.5,0.5,1.5,3,8,20,50]:
       add(np.exp(lam*t), f'exp{lam}')
    for b in [1,2,3,5,10,20]:
       add(1.0 + b*t)
       add(1.0 - 0.5*b*t) # may negative; clip
    # powers
    te=t+1e-4
    one=1-te+1e-4
    for p in [0.2,0.5,1,2,4,8]:
       add(te**p)
       add(one**p)
    for p in [0.5,1,2,4]:
       add(te**p * one**p) # symmetric bad maybe
    # sigmoids
    for mu,s in [(0.3,10),(0.3,50),(0.4,100),(0.6,100),(0.7,50)]:
       add(0.1 + 0.9/(1+np.exp(-s*(t-mu))))
       add(0.1 + 0.9/(1+np.exp(s*(t-mu))))
    # quadratic skewed
    for a in [1,2,3,5]:
       add(1 + a*t + a*(t-0.5)**2)
       add(1 + a*t - a*(t-0.5)**2)
    # random
    for sig in [0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0]:
       for tilt in [-2,-1,0,1,2]:
          add(np.exp(tilt*t + sig*rng.standard_normal(n)))
    # zeros random mask? add binary random densities
    for d in [0.5,0.7,0.8]:
       a=(rng.random(n) < d).astype(float)+0.05
       add(a)
    if parent available and length n: add(parent*(1+0.1*noise))
    return out
```
Maybe too many (100). We can evaluate each (FFT) to pick top. 100 FFTs 16384 ~ maybe 1s. Fine. But memory list 100*6733=5.4MB. Good.
Need use parent length maybe not n. If parent length differs, we can resample? Simpler if parent length == n use; else use 6733. For resample, could `np.interp`, but not necessary. We'll set n = parent_len if 512<=parent_len<=12000 else 6733. If using parent length, our `t` length n.

```
def build_constraint_matrix(f, L, R):
    n=len(f); m=2*n-1; B=len(L)
    pf=np.empty(n+1); pf[0]=0; pf[1:]=np.cumsum(f)
    k=np.arange(m, dtype=np.int64)
    A=np.empty((m,B), dtype=np.float64)
    lo_buf=np.empty(m, dtype=np.int64)
    hi_buf=np.empty(m, dtype=np.int64)
    for i in range(B):
       np.subtract(k, R[i], out=lo_buf)
       lo_buf < 0? In-place: `np.maximum(lo_buf, 0, out=lo_buf)` but lo_buf is int; use `np.clip(lo_buf, 0, n-1, out=lo_buf)`.
       np.subtract(k, L[i], out=hi_buf)
       np.clip(hi_buf, 0, n-1, out=hi_buf)
       valid = lo_buf <= hi_buf
       # A[valid,i] = pf[hi_buf[valid]+1] - pf[lo_buf[valid]]
       A[valid, i] = pf[hi_buf[valid] + 1] - pf[lo_buf[valid]]
       A[~valid, i] = 0.0
    return A
```

`lp_direction(f, c, blocks_L, blocks_R, block_lengths)`:
```
def lp_direction(f, c, L, R, lengths):
    if linprog is None: return None
    Mmax = float(np.max(c))
    if Mmax <=0: return None
    n=len(f); m=2*n-1; B=len(lengths)
    A=build_constraint_matrix(f,L,R)
    b=np.full(m, Mmax)
    obj=-lengths.astype(float)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * B
    try:
       res=linprog(c=obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or not res.success:
       # try ปรับ? no
       return None
    y=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
    y=np.maximum(y,0)
    total=float(np.dot(y, lengths))
    if total <= 1e-12: return None
    g=np.empty(n); 
    for i in range(B): g[L[i]:R[i]+1] = y[i]
    g=np.maximum(g,0)
    tot=g.sum()
    if tot<=0: return None
    S0=float(f.sum())
    g *= S0/tot
    return g
```
Potential LP memory/time. If B=512, A 13k*512=6.9M floats 55MB. `linprog` may copy; memory okay. If B=1024 110MB maybe. Use B up to 384? Let's choose phases [64,128,256,384]. 384 ->41MB. Good detail. Maybe 512 okay. Use [64,128,256,512] and if memory error fallback. Wrap in try.

```
def make_blocks(n,B):
    if B <1: B=1

    lengths = np.full(B, base, dtype=np.int64)
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def fft_convolution(a):
    n = len(a)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_pow2(m)
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)[:m]
    # Remove tiny negative numerical artifacts.  Max is what matters.
    if c.max() > 0:
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def score_from_c(a, c):
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * len(a) * float(np.max(c)) / (s * s))


def fast_score(a):
    return score_from_c(a, fft_convolution(a))


def normalize_positive(a, S0, floor_frac=1e-9):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = np.minimum(a, 1e150)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        a = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
        n = 1
    tot = float(np.sum(a))
    if tot <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(tot):
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        tot = float(n)
    a = a * (float(S0) / tot)
    flo = max(1e-12, floor_frac * float(S0) / n)
    a = np.maximum(a, flo)
    a = a * (float(S0) / float(np.sum(a)))
    return a


def topL_proposal(a, c, L, gamma, rng):
    n = len(a)
    M = len(c)
    if L <= 1:
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        L = int(max(2, min(L, M - 1)))
        idx = np.argpartition(c, M - L)[-L:]

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for kk in idx:
        k = int(kk)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if lo <= hi:
            # g[i] += 2 a[k-i] for i in [lo,hi]
            vals = a[k - hi:k - lo + 1][::-1]
            g[lo:hi + 1] += 2.0 * vals

    gm = float(np.mean(g))
    z = g - gm
    scale = float(np.max(np.abs(z)))
    if scale <= 1e-15 or not math.isfinite(scale):
        # Fallback: tiny smoothed random step.
        cand = a * (1.0 + 1e-4 * rng.standard_normal(n))
        return cand

    z = z / scale
    z = np.clip(z, -3.0, 3.0)
    cand = a * np.exp(-float(gamma) * z)
    cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
    return cand


def run_topL(a0, S0, deadline, rng, verbose=True):
    a = normalize_positive(a0, S0)
    n = len(a)
    cur_c = fft_convolution(a)
    cur_score = score_from_c(a, cur_c)

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    if not math.isfinite(best_score):
        return best, best_score

    it = 0
    no_imp = 0
    L_cycle = [1, 1, 2, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16]
    floor = 1e-9 * float(S0) / n

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        L = L_cycle[it % len(L_cycle)]
        if no_imp > 300:
            L = min(32, L * 2)
        gamma = (0.08 + 0.40 / (1.0 + 0.00025 * it)) * (0.75 + 0.5 * rng.random())
        cand = topL_proposal(a, cur_c, L, gamma, rng)
        cand = np.maximum(cand, floor)
        cand = cand * (float(S0) / float(np.sum(cand)))

        cand_c = fft_convolution(cand)
        cand_score = score_from_c(cand, cand_c)

        accept = False
        if math.isfinite(cand_score):
            if cand_score <= cur_score:
                accept = True
            elif no_imp > 80 and cand_score < cur_score + 0.01 and rng.random() < 0.08:
                accept = True

        improved_best = False
        if accept and math.isfinite(cand_score):
            a = cand
            cur_c = cand_c
            cur_score = cand_score
            if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = cur_score
                best = a.copy()
                no_imp = 0
                improved_best = True
            else:
                no_imp += 1
        else:
            no_imp += 1

        if no_imp > 600:
            sigma = 0.08 + 0.45 * rng.random()
            base = best if rng.random() < 0.75 else (a if len(a) == best_size_local else best)
            a = base * np.exp(rng.standard_normal(n) * sigma)
            a = normalize_positive(a, S0)
            cur_c = fft_convolution(a)
            cur_score = score_from_c(a, cur_c)
            no_imp = 0

        it += 1
        if verbose and (it % 2000 == 0):
            print("topL it=%d best=%.8f cur=%.8f" % (it, best_score, cur_score), flush=True)

    return best, best_score


def make_blocks(n, B):
    if B < 1:
        B = 1
    if B > n:
        B = n
    base = n // B
    rem = n - base * B
    lengths = np.full(B, base, dtype=np.int64)
    if rem > 0:
        lengths[:rem] += 1
    L = np.repeat(np.arange(B, dtype=np.int64), lengths)
    R = L + lengths - 1
    return L, R, lengths


def build_constraint_matrix(f, L, R):
    n = len(f)
    M = 2 * n - 1
    B = len(L)
    pf = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    pf[0] = 0.0
    pf[1:] = np.cumsum(f, dtype=np.float64)

    k = np.arange(M, dtype=np.int64)
    lo_buf = np.empty(M, dtype=np.int64)
    hi_buf = np.empty(M, dtype=np.int64)
    A = np.zeros((M, B), dtype=np.float64)
    n_minus_1 = n - 1

    for i in range(B):
        np.subtract(k, int(R[i]), out=lo_buf)
        np.maximum(lo_buf, 0, out=lo_buf)
        np.subtract(k, int(L[i]), out=hi_buf)
        np.minimum(hi_buf, n_minus_1, out=hi_buf)
        valid = lo_buf <= hi_buf
        if np.any(valid):
            A[valid, i] = pf[hi_buf[valid] + 1] - pf[lo_buf[valid]]
    return A


def lp_direction(f, c, L, R, lengths):
    if linprog is None:
        return None

    Mmax = float(np.max(c))
    if Mmax <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(Mmax):
        return None

    n = len(f)
    M = 2 * n - 1
    B = len(lengths)

    try:
        A = build_constraint_matrix(f, L, R)
        b = np.full(M, Mmax, dtype=np.float64)
        obj = -lengths.astype(np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * B
        res = linprog(
            c=obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"disp": False},
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None

    y = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    y = np.where(np.isfinite(y), y, 0.0)
    y = np.maximum(y, 0.0)

    mean_y = float(np.mean(y))
    if mean_y > 0.0:
        y = np.minimum(y, 10.0 * mean_y + 1e-12)

    total = float(np.dot(y, lengths))
    if total <= 1e-12:
        return None

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(B):
        g[int(L[i]):int(R[i]) + 1] = y[i]

    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    g = normalize_positive(g, float(np.sum(f)))
    g = 0.95 * g + 0.05 * (float(np.sum(f)) / n) * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    g = normalize_positive(g, float(np.sum(f)))
    return g


def run_lp_sota(a0, S0, deadline, rng, restart_pool, verbose=True):
    global best_size_local
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(0.0, deadline - start_time)
    f = normalize_positive(a0, S0)
    n = len(f)
    best_size_local = n

    cur_c = fft_convolution(f)
    cur_score = score_from_c(f, cur_c)
    best = f.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    if not math.isfinite(best_score):
        return best, best_score

    if linprog is None or total_budget < 60.0:
        return best, best_score

    # B task allocation.  Enough time for coarse-to-fine block SOTA.
    phases = [
        (64, 0.10, 1.30),
        (128, 0.12, 1.30),
        (256, 0.22, 1.00),
        (512, 0.30, 0.70),
    ]
    # Remaining fraction is used for continuation at final B if possible.
    t_values = [0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.40, 0.80, 1.00]
    memory_fail = False
    cumulative = 0.0

    for phase_idx, (B, frac, step_scale) in enumerate(phases):
        if memory_fail:
            break
        phase_end = start_time + (cumulative + frac) * total_budget
        cumulative += frac
        if phase_end > deadline:
            phase_end = deadline
        if phase_end <= time.monotonic():
            continue
        if B > n:
            B = n

        blocks = make_blocks(n, B)
        L, R, lengths = blocks
        fail_streak = 0
        stagnation = 0
        it = 0

        # Start each phase from the current global best to avoid drift.
        f = best.copy()
        cur_c = fft_convolution(f)
        cur_score = score_from_c(f, cur_c)

        while time.monotonic() < phase_end:
            if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = cur_score
                best = f.copy()
                stagnation = 0

            try:
                g = lp_direction(f, cur_c, L, R, lengths)
            except MemoryError:
                memory_fail = True
                fail_streak += 1
                break
            except Exception:
                g = None
                fail_streak += 1

            if g is None:
                fail_streak += 1
                if fail_streak >= 6:
                    break
                # Use a quick top-L step to change state before trying LP again.
                gamma = 0.10 + 0.10 * rng.random()
                f = topL_proposal(f, cur_c, 3, gamma, rng)
                f = normalize_positive(f, S0)
                cur_c = fft_convolution(f)
                cur_score = score_from_c(f, cur_c)
                it += 1
                continue
            fail_streak = 0

            best_f = f
            best_c = cur_c
            best_sc = cur_score
            for t in t_values:
                tt = float(t) / max(1.0, step_scale)
                tt = min(1.0, max(0.01, tt))
                cand = (1.0 - tt) * f + tt * g
                cand = normalize_positive(cand, S0)
                cc = fft_convolution(cand)
                sc = score_from_c(cand, cc)
                if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    best_sc = sc
                    best_c = cc
                    best_f = cand

            if best_sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
                f = best_f
                cur_c = best_c
                cur_score = best_sc
                if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score = cur_score
                    best = f.copy()
                    stagnation = 0
                else:
                    stagnation += 1
            else:
                stagnation += 1
                # If the LP direction is not improving, perturb and try again.
                if stagnation > 8:
                    if rng.random() < 0.70:
                        base = best
                    elif restart_pool is not None and len(restart_pool) > 0:
                        base = restart_pool[int(rng.integers(0, len(restart_pool)))]
                    else:
                        base = best
                    sigma = 0.05 + 0.35 * rng.random()
                    f = normalize_positive(base * np.exp(rng.standard_normal(n) * sigma), S0)
                    cur_c = fft_convolution(f)
                    cur_score = score_from_c(f, cur_c)
                    if cur_score > best_score * 1.05:
                        f = best.copy()
                        cur_c = fft_convolution(f)
                        cur_score = score_from_c(f, cur_c)
                    stagnation = 0

            it += 1
            if verbose and (it % 8 == 0):
                print("LP B=%d it=%d best=%.8f cur=%.8f" % (B, it, best_score, cur_score), flush=True)

        # At phase end, restore best state for the next phase.
        if cur_score > best_score:
            f = best.copy()
            cur_c = fft_convolution(f)
            cur_score = score_from_c(f, cur_score) if False else score_from_c(f, cur_c)

        if verbose:
            print("LP phase B=%d done best=%.8f" % (B, best_score), flush=True)

    # Return the best found by LP; caller may continue local search.
    return best, best_score


def score_and_sort(initials):
    scored = []
    for i, a in enumerate(initials):
        c = fft_convolution(a)
        s = score_from_c(a, c)
        if math.isfinite(s):
            scored.append((float(s), i, a))
    scored.sort()
    return [item[2] for item in scored]


def generate_initials(n, S0, rng):
    out = []
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)

    # Exponential tilts.
    for lam in (-12.0, -6.0, -3.0, -1.5, -0.7, 0.7, 1.5, 3.0, 6.0, 12.0, 30.0):
        out.append(normalize_positive(np.exp(lam * t), S0))

    # Linear ramps.
    for b in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0, 20.0, 80.0):
        out.append(normalize_positive(1.0 + b * t, S0))
        out.append(normalize_positive(1.0 - 0.5 * b * t, S0))
        out.append(normalize_positive(1.0 + b * (1.0 - t), S0))

    # Power shapes.
    te = np.maximum(t, 1e-4)
    oe = np.maximum(1.0 - t, 1e-4)
    for p in (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0):
        out.append(normalize_positive(te ** p, S0))
        out.append(normalize_positive(oe ** p, S0))
        out.append(normalize_positive(te ** p + 0.5 * oe ** p, S0))
        out.append(normalize_positive(0.5 * te ** p + oe ** p, S0))

    # Sigmoids / soft steps.
    for mu, sh in (
        (0.25, 8.0), (0.25, 30.0), (0.25, 80.0),
        (0.35, 10.0), (0.35, 40.0),
        (0.50, 10.0), (0.50, 30.0), (0.50, 80.0),
        (0.65, 10.0), (0.65, 40.0),
        (0.75, 8.0), (0.75, 30.0), (0.75, 80.0),
    ):
        s1 = 0.1 + 0.9 / (1.0 + np.exp(-sh * (t - mu)))
        s2 = 0.1 + 0.9 / (1.0 + np.exp(sh * (t - mu)))
        out.append(normalize_positive(s1, S0))
        out.append(normalize_positive(s2, S0))

    # Skewed quadratics.
    for a in (1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0):
        out.append(normalize_positive(1.0 + a * t + a * (t - 0.5) ** 2, S0))
        out.append(normalize_positive(1.0 + a * t - a * (t - 0.5) ** 2, S0))
        out.append(normalize_positive(1.0 + a * (1.0 - t) + a * (t - 0.5) ** 2, S0))

    # Two-level steps.
    for alpha in (0.25, 0.35, 0.45, 0.55, 0.65, 0.75):
        for r in (0.2, 0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.5):
            a1 = np.where(t < alpha, 1.0, r).astype(np.float64)
            a2 = np.where(t < alpha, r, 1.0).astype(np.float64)
            out.append(normalize_positive(a1, S0))
            out.append(normalize_positive(a2, S0))

    # Random positive with tilts.
    for sig in (0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        for tilt in (-4.0, -2.0, -1.0, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
            out.append(normalize_positive(np.exp(tilt * t + sig * rng.standard_normal(n)), S0))

    # Random binary-ish sequences: keep mass but structured holes.
    for d in (0.5, 0.65, 0.8, 0.9):
        a = (rng.random(n) < d).astype(np.float64) + 0.05
        out.append(normalize_positive(a, S0))
        # Shift holes to one side.
        mask = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        mask[: int(round(d * n))] = 1.0
        mask[int(round(d * n)):] = 0.05
        out.append(normalize_positive(mask, S0))
        mask = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        mask[: int(round((1.0 - d) * n))] = 0.05
        mask[int(round((1.0 - d) * n)):] = 1.0
        out.append(normalize_positive(mask, S0))

    # Use the supplied global construction if its length matches.
    p = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    if p is not None:
        pa = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if pa.size == n and np.any(np.isfinite(pa)):
            pa = np.where(np.isfinite(pa), pa, 0.0)
            pa = np.maximum(pa, 0.0)
            if float(np.sum(pa)) > 0.0:
                out.append(normalize_positive(pa + 1e-4, S0))
                for sig in (0.05, 0.15, 0.30, 0.60):
                    out.append(normalize_positive(pa * np.exp(sig * rng.standard_normal(n)), S0))
                # Asymmetric tilt on parent.
                for lam in (-1.0, -0.2, 0.2, 1.0):
                    out.append(normalize_positive(pa * np.exp(lam * t), S0))

    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    global best_size_local
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, budget - 5.0)

    # Choose a practical length.  Use the supplied construction when possible.
    p = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    n = 6733
    if p is not None:
        pl = np.asarray(p)
        if pl.ndim == 1 and 512 <= pl.size <= 12000:
            n = int(pl.size)

    S0 = math.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    best_size_local = n

    # Phase 1: good diverse initial points.
    initial_budget = min(45.0, max(5.0, 0.03 * budget))
    initial_deadline = t0 + initial_budget
    initials = generate_initials(n, S0, rng)
    if time.monotonic() > initial_deadline:
        initials = initials[:5]
    sorted_starts = score_and_sort(initials)
    if len(sorted_starts) == 0:
        sorted_starts = [normalize_positive(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), S0)]

    # Add a deterministic safe fallback for tiny budgets or failures: [2,1].
    fallback = np.array([2.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    fallback_score = 16.0 / 9.0

    best = sorted_starts[0].copy()
    best_score = score_from_c(best, fft_convolution(best))
    if not math.isfinite(best_score):
        best = fallback.copy()
        best_score = fallback_score

    # Keep a small pool of promising random-ish starts for restarts.
    restart_pool = []
    for a in sorted_starts[:5]:
        restart_pool.append(a.copy())

    # Phase 2: block-restricted SOTA LP search.
    if linprog is not None and deadline - time.monotonic() > 60.0:
        lp_deadline = t0 + min(
            deadline,
            t0 + initial_budget + 0.82 * max(0.0, deadline - (t0 + initial_budget)),
        )
        best, best_score = run_lp_sota(
            best, S0, lp_deadline, rng, restart_pool, verbose=True
        )
        if not math.isfinite(best_score):
            best = sorted_starts[0].copy()
            best_score = score_from_c(best, fft_convolution(best))

    # Phase 3: fast top-L local search for refinement.
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 20.0:
        best, best_score = run_topL(best, S0, deadline, rng, verbose=True)

    # If everything pathological fails, return a valid small sequence.
    if not math.isfinite(best_score) or len(best) == 0:
        best = fallback
        best_score = fallback_score

    # Never return the old 2.0 plateau if we can safely do a tiny better sequence.
    if best_score >= 2.0:
        # A length-2 optimal-ish sequence is a guaranteed improvement over 2.0.
        if fallback_score < best_score:
            best = fallback
            best_score = fallback_score

    # Final sanitize.
    best = np.asarray(best, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    best = np.where(np.isfinite(best), best, 0.0)
    best = np.clip(best, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(best))
    if s < 0.01:
        best = fllaseback if False else fallback
        best = np.clip(best, 0.0, 1000.0)
    else:
        # Preserve score scale; values remain below 1000 for our S0.
        best = best * (math.sqrt(2.0 * float(len(best))) / s)
        best = np.clip(best, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s2 = float(np.sum(best))
        if s2 < 0.01:
            best = fallback

    try:
        ev = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
        if ev is not None:
            official = ev([float(x) for x in best])
            if math.isfinite(official):
                print("FINAL official score: %.8f" % official, flush=True)
                if official < best_score:
                    best_score = official
    except Exception:
        pass

    print("FINAL returned length=%d best internaL score estimate=%.8f" % (len(best), best_score), flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in best]
```
